# Skills reorganize report: github.com/gke-labs/kube-agents

Generated 2026-10-14 02:05 UTC by SSS. This is a local working document in
`.sss/reports/`; it is not committed. Sections 2 to 6 answer one question each.

## 1. Summary

We looked at 112 skills in this repo (all local; SSS manages none yet). This run was on a pull-request branch under review in which two skills changed: fleet-upgrade-verification, whose readiness check grew a registry of per-entry rules from the upgrade-failure catalogue with an audit-log reader and a first rule (a zonal control plane, reported as a risk that never blocks), and the shipped copy of gke-upgrades, whose appended section now points at that registry. We wrote new coverage summaries for those two and scored their 221 pairs; the other 5,995 pair scores came from the cache. The rescoring moved nothing: the two skills remain 3 stars apart (declared counterparts, one executes and one advises), the shipped and upstream gke-upgrades copies remain 1 star apart, and no new pair fell to 2 stars or lower, so the changed skills add no new overlap with the rest of the set. Across all 6,216 pairs, 29 score 2 stars or lower, 16 of them byte-identical upstream-and-shipped copies of a gke-* skill. What the changed skills add: an agent can now ask one script for drain-blocking PDBs, maintenance exclusions and windows, node-pool skew and the catalogue rules in one pass, per member, with per-kind read errors so a cut pod list costs only the rules that need pods, and gke-upgrades tells its reader to carry those rows into the plan rather than check them by hand. What they break or risk: the new fleet-upgrade-verification text contradicts its own scripts once (it says three reads are skipped after a failed PDB read where the code and its own list say four) and one new sentence about a rule's own reads is easy to misread, both in lines this pull request added; with --readiness, exit 0 no longer means every read succeeded, which fleet-upgrade-verification states but gke-upgrades does not; the SKILL.md spends a paragraph on the audit-log read and the "other blockers" column although the only registered rule reads the cluster record alone and can never block; and the appended section's older claim that the script reads with gcloud container only and writes one file still disagrees with the script, which this change did not touch. Everything else in this report is unchanged from the run at the merge base: the gke-* skills are generated from third_party/google-skills/ plus overlay patches and SSS does not yet know that tree is a source, so the duplicates and most conflicts are that pipeline seen from outside, and the five hand-maintained copies under agents/cluster/skills/ still drift from the shipped copies. A second, sceptical pass re-read every finding against its quoted evidence: of 65 findings it kept 65, changed 5 (it dropped the unfinished-sentence claim from the new fleet-upgrade-verification finding, downgraded the new gke-upgrades finding to likely and the drift-detection finding to unsure, and replaced two non-verbatim quotes), and deleted none. Nothing was changed in the repo: this run was report-only by request.

## 2. Conflicts

These skills disagree. An agent that loads both will get two answers.

### conf-ff5268: capacity-obtainability, gke-stockout-investigator

Both skills load into the same platform profile; one bans the word "guarantee" for reservations in any report and the other tells the agent a reservation exists "to guarantee compute capacity", so a stockout PR or notification describes a reservation as a guarantee and a capacity report built from the same Diagnostics B text fails the eval that grades the word out.

Evidence:
- `capacity-obtainability/SKILL.md:361`: "Check if any zonal reservations are available for the target workload's machine type; a reservation holds capacity for it (the report must not describe this as a guarantee):"
- `capacity-obtainability/SKILL.md:50`: "the word "guarantee" about capacity, allocations, or scheduling anywhere"
- `gke-stockout-investigator/SKILL.md:159`: "Check if any zonal reservations are available for the target workload's machine type to guarantee compute capacity:"

Options:
1. Keep A, retire B.
2. Keep B, retire A.
3. Keep both, and write a local patch that reconciles them.
4. Open an issue upstream, and patch locally meanwhile.

**Recommendation** (likely): Patch gke-stockout-investigator Diagnostics B to the capacity-obtainability wording ("holds capacity", not "guarantee") so both copies of the shared diagnostics say the same thing. (Which side is right: a — the repository's eval bench/tasks/obtainability-design-quota-vs-capacity/task.yaml (line 73: 'The skill bans the word "guarantee"') grades the word out, and capacity-obtainability line 361 is the corrected form of the same Diagnostics B sentence the stockout skill still carries.)

Decision needed: yes

### conf-e96220: gke-workload-troubleshooting (agents/platform/skills/gke-workload-troubleshooting), gke-workload-troubleshooting (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-troubleshooting)

The two copies give a different default {issue_time} for the same input (no time given), so the +/-30 minute window at Step 2 lands in different places: centred on now it misses a crash older than 30 minutes and the Cloud Logging query returns nothing.

Evidence:
- `gke-workload-troubleshooting (agents/platform/skills/gke-workload-troubleshooting)/SKILL.md:53`: "-   **No Time Provided (Default)**: Use current system time as"
- `gke-workload-troubleshooting (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-troubleshooting)/SKILL.md:36`: "2. If there are crashing or pending containers, check their state transition timestamps (e.g. `status.containerStatuses[*].lastState.terminated.finishedAt` or `status.startTime`) and use that transition time as $T$."

Options:
1. Keep A, retire B.
2. Keep B, retire A.
3. Keep both, and write a local patch that reconciles them.
4. Open an issue upstream, and patch locally meanwhile.

**Recommendation** (likely): Keep the Platform Agent copy, patch the Cluster Agent copy (option 3); the two load into different profiles, so each agent sees only its own answer. Detail: Carry the cluster copy's pod-timestamp default into the platform copy as an overlay patch (and upstream), so both agents centre the window on the observed failure. (Which side is right: b — the window exists to bracket the failure, and the cluster copy anchors it on the pod's observed transition timestamp while the upstream default anchors it on the current time; a crash from two hours ago falls outside a window centred on now (verified against the Step 2 timestamp-bounded query in both files).)

Decision needed: yes

### conf-e0d880: gke-storage (agents/cluster/skills/gke-storage), gke-storage (agents/platform/skills/gke-storage)

(1) A Cluster Agent that follows its copy writes a Filestore PVC with storageClassName standard-rwm, a StorageClass GKE does not ship, so the claim stays Pending with a 'storageclass not found' event. (2) The platform copy says premium-rwo is GKE's built-in zonal pd-ssd class while the cluster copy's example creates a StorageClass of that same name with replication-type regional-pd, so an agent reading the cluster copy believes premium-rwo gives regional replication, and applying the example collides with the addon-managed built-in object.

Evidence:
- `gke-storage (agents/platform/skills/gke-storage)/SKILL.md:47`: "[1] `standard-rwx` | Filestore (Basic HDD) | Shared NFS"
- `gke-storage (agents/platform/skills/gke-storage)/SKILL.md:93`: "[1]   storageClassName: standard-rwx"
- `gke-storage (agents/cluster/skills/gke-storage)/SKILL.md:55`: "[1]   storageClassName: standard-rwm # Pre-defined for Filestore"
- `gke-storage (agents/platform/skills/gke-storage)/SKILL.md:46`: "[2] `premium-rwo`  | `pd-ssd`              | High IOPS, databases"
- `gke-storage (agents/platform/skills/gke-storage)/SKILL.md:56`: "[2]   name: fast-regional"
- `gke-storage (agents/cluster/skills/gke-storage)/SKILL.md:26`: "[2]   name: premium-rwo"
- `gke-storage (agents/cluster/skills/gke-storage)/SKILL.md:30`: "[2]   replication-type: regional-pd"

Options:
1. Keep A, retire B.
2. Keep B, retire A.
3. Keep both, and write a local patch that reconciles them.
4. Open an issue upstream, and patch locally meanwhile.

**Recommendation** (likely): Keep the Platform Agent copy, patch the Cluster Agent copy (option 3); the two load into different profiles, so each agent sees only its own answer. Detail: (1) Patch the cluster copy's PVC example to storageClassName: standard-rwx, the name the platform copy and GKE use; right side: a — GKE's built-in Filestore CSI StorageClasses are standard-rwx, premium-rwx, enterprise-rwx and zonal-rwx (GKE Filestore CSI driver docs); standard-rwm appears nowhere else in the repo and the platform copy is byte-identical to the google/skills upstream. (2) Rename the cluster copy's custom StorageClass example away from the built-in name premium-rwo, matching the platform copy's fast-regional; right side: a — premium-rwo is a GKE-provisioned built-in StorageClass backed by zonal pd-ssd (GKE persistent-volumes docs; same table in the google/skills upstream), so a custom regional class needs its own name as the platform copy's fast-regional example does.

Decision needed: yes

### conf-323599: gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades), gke-upgrades (third_party/google-skills/gke-upgrades)

(1) The two runbook templates relax a blocking PDB with different patch bodies; the upstream body leaves minAvailable in place, and the API server rejects a PDB that sets both minAvailable and maxUnavailable, so the drain stays blocked on any PDB that uses minAvailable. (2) The copies disagree on how to restore a relaxed PDB: upstream re-applies the `kubectl get -o yaml` backup, which carries the pre-patch resourceVersion and is answered with a 409 conflict, so the agent following upstream reports the restore done while the PDB stays at maxUnavailable 100%. (3) The copies disagree on whether a blue-green node-pool upgrade pauses when the maintenance window closes, so an agent reading the upstream SKILL.md tells the operator to widen the window for a blue-green pool that is not paused by it. (4) The GPU-driver rollback step uses different gcloud commands in the two copies; `gcloud container node-pools upgrade` does not exist, so the upstream command fails with 'Invalid choice: upgrade' while production is blocked.

Evidence:
- `gke-upgrades (third_party/google-skills/gke-upgrades)/references/runbook-template.md:164`: "[1]   --type merge -p '{"spec":{"maxUnavailable":"100%"}}'"
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/references/runbook-template.md:164`: "[1]   --type merge -p '{"spec":{"minAvailable":null,"maxUnavailable":"100%"}}'"
- `gke-upgrades (third_party/google-skills/gke-upgrades)/references/runbook-template.md:171`: "[2] kubectl apply -f /tmp/pdb-backup-TIMESTAMP.yaml"
- `gke-upgrades (third_party/google-skills/gke-upgrades)/references/troubleshooting.md:41`: "[2] kubectl apply -f pdb-backup.yaml"
- `gke-upgrades (third_party/google-skills/gke-upgrades)/SKILL.md:176`: "[2] **Any runbook that relaxes a safety control must restore it in the same runbook.** This applies above all to PDBs during a node-pool migration or rollback: back the PDBs up before draining, and make re-applying them a numbered step with its own verification, not a closing remark. A runbook that patches `maxUnavailable: 100%` to unblock a drain and never reverts it leaves the cluster without disruption protection, and the gap is invisible until the next voluntary eviction. The same rule covers maintenance exclusions, cordons, and autoscaler `minNodes` overrides added to get through the procedure."
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/references/runbook-template.md:171`: "[2] #    `kubectl apply` the backup: its stale resourceVersion is rejected with a"
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/references/runbook-template.md:174`: "[2]   --type merge -p '{"spec":{"maxUnavailable":null,"minAvailable":ORIGINAL_MIN_AVAILABLE}}'"
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/references/troubleshooting.md:44`: "[2]   --type merge -p '{"spec":{"maxUnavailable":null,"minAvailable":ORIGINAL_MIN_AVAILABLE}}'"
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/SKILL.md:176`: "[2] **Any runbook that relaxes a safety control must restore it in the same runbook.** This applies above all to PDBs during a node-pool migration or rollback: back the PDBs up before draining, and make restoring them by patch a numbered step with its own verification, not a closing remark. A runbook that patches `maxUnavailable: 100%` to unblock a drain and never reverts it leaves the cluster without disruption protection, and the gap is invisible until the next voluntary eviction. The same rule covers maintenance exclusions, cordons, and autoscaler `minNodes` overrides added to get through the procedure."
- `gke-upgrades (third_party/google-skills/gke-upgrades)/SKILL.md:182`: "[3] - **Silent Pause Behavior:** If a maintenance window closes before an upgrade (auto or manual) completes, GKE intentionally pauses the rollout to prevent disruption outside allowed times."
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/SKILL.md:182`: "[3] - **Silent Pause Behavior:** If a maintenance window closes before a surge upgrade (auto or manual) completes, GKE intentionally pauses the rollout to prevent disruption outside allowed times. A blue-green node-pool upgrade does not pause: it continues past the window to completion (`references/troubleshooting.md` §11), so for a blue-green pool the window is not the cause and the rest of this section does not apply."
- `gke-upgrades (third_party/google-skills/gke-upgrades)/references/troubleshooting.md:191`: "[4]    gcloud container node-pools upgrade NODE_POOL_NAME \"
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/references/troubleshooting.md:198`: "[4]    gcloud container clusters upgrade CLUSTER_NAME \"
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/references/troubleshooting.md:199`: "[4]      --node-pool NODE_POOL_NAME \"

### conf-baf375: gke-workload-security (agents/platform/skills/gke-workload-security), gke-workload-security (third_party/google-skills/gke-workload-security)

(1) An agent following the one-command copy stops after enabling the NetworkPolicy addon on the control plane, so enforcement never reaches the nodes and the default-deny policy applied next is silently ignored on a non-Dataplane-V2 cluster. The prerequisite line describes when Gateway API is on by default in a way that no longer matches GKE, so an agent may skip enabling it on a Standard cluster or try to enable it where it is always on.

Evidence:
- `gke-service-networking (agents/platform/skills/gke-service-networking)/SKILL.md:25`: "**Prerequisites**: Gateway API must be enabled on the cluster (enabled by"
- `gke-service-networking (agents/platform/skills/gke-service-networking)/SKILL.md:26`: "default in GKE 1.24+)."
- `gke-service-networking (third_party/google-skills/gke-service-networking)/SKILL.md:25`: "**Prerequisites**: Gateway API must be enabled on the cluster (enabled by"
- `gke-service-networking (third_party/google-skills/gke-service-networking)/SKILL.md:26`: "default in GKE 1.24+)."

**Recommendation** (likely): State what the GKE docs say now: GKE Gateway is always enabled on Autopilot clusters, and Standard clusters need `gcloud container clusters update CLUSTER_NAME --location=CLUSTER_LOCATION --gateway-api=standard`; 1.24 is the minimum supported version, not the version at which it became a default.

Decision needed: yes

### old-063060: gke-platform-security (agents/platform/skills/gke-platform-security), gke-platform-security (third_party/google-skills/gke-platform-security), gke-workload-security (agents/platform/skills/gke-workload-security), gke-workload-security (third_party/google-skills/gke-workload-security), gke-workload-security (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-security)

gcloud container clusters update has no --enable-gke-sandbox flag, so the command fails; GKE Sandbox (gVisor) is set per node pool with --sandbox type=gvisor.

Evidence:
- `gke-platform-security (agents/platform/skills/gke-platform-security)/SKILL.md:116`: "# Enable GKE Sandbox (gVisor) runtime on an existing cluster"
- `gke-platform-security (agents/platform/skills/gke-platform-security)/SKILL.md:117`: "gcloud container clusters update <CLUSTER_NAME> --region <REGION> \"
- `gke-platform-security (agents/platform/skills/gke-platform-security)/SKILL.md:118`: "  --enable-gke-sandbox \"
- `gke-platform-security (agents/platform/skills/gke-platform-security)/SKILL.md:8`: "  enabling Shielded Nodes, GKE Sandbox cluster enablement, GKE IAM roles, and"
- `gke-platform-security (third_party/google-skills/gke-platform-security)/SKILL.md:116`: "# Enable GKE Sandbox (gVisor) runtime on an existing cluster"
- `gke-platform-security (third_party/google-skills/gke-platform-security)/SKILL.md:118`: "  --enable-gke-sandbox \"
- `gke-workload-security (agents/platform/skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:153`: "(`--enable-gke-sandbox`) at the cluster control plane level are platform-level"
- `gke-workload-security (third_party/google-skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:122`: "(`--enable-gke-sandbox`) at the cluster control plane level are platform-level"
- `gke-workload-security (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:145`: "--enable-gke-sandbox \"

**Recommendation** (confident): Replace the cluster-update command with the node-pool form (gcloud container node-pools create <POOL> --cluster <CLUSTER> --sandbox type=gvisor) in every copy; fix upstream and overlay meanwhile.

Decision needed: yes

### old-946718: gke-workload-security (third_party/google-skills/gke-workload-security), gke-workload-security (agents/platform/skills/gke-workload-security), gke-workload-security (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-security)

The manifest uses the open-source CSI driver names (provider: gcp, secrets-store.csi.k8s.io) while the linked prerequisite is the GKE add-on, which needs provider: gke and secrets-store-gke.csi.k8s.io; the mount fails on a cluster set up as the skill says.

Evidence:
- `gke-workload-security (third_party/google-skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:159`: "**Prerequisites**: Secret Manager CSI driver must be enabled on the cluster."
- `gke-workload-security (third_party/google-skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:169`: "  provider: gcp"
- `gke-workload-security (third_party/google-skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:189`: "        driver: secrets-store.csi.k8s.io"
- `gke-workload-security (third_party/google-skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:242`: "-   [Google Secret Manager CSI Driver](https://cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/secret-manager-managed-csi-component)"
- `gke-workload-security (agents/platform/skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:200`: "provider: gcp"
- `gke-workload-security (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:181`: "provider: gcp"

**Recommendation** (confident): The linked GKE-managed Secret Manager add-on (`gcloud container clusters update --enable-secret-manager`) uses `provider: gke` in the SecretProviderClass and `driver: secrets-store-gke.csi.k8s.io` in the pod volume; `provider: gcp` with `secrets-store.csi.k8s.io` is the self-installed open-source Secrets Store CSI driver, which that page describes as the path to migrate away from, so the manifest should use the add-on values (or state which driver each pair belongs to).

Decision needed: yes

### old-cae437: review-security-k8s-agents-audit-logs

A reviewer searching manifests for a resource kind named AuditPolicy finds nothing, because no such Kubernetes kind exists; the repo's own policy file uses the real kind.

Evidence:
- `review-security-k8s-agents-audit-logs/SKILL.md:11`: "- **Audit Policy**: Ensure `AuditPolicy` captures all agent API requests (minimum `Metadata` level, prefer `RequestResponse` for mutations)."

**Recommendation** (likely): Name the resource as it exists: a `kind: Policy` object in `apiVersion: audit.k8s.io/v1` (the repo's reference is `k8s-operator/config/audit/audit-policy.yaml`), and note that on GKE the API-server audit policy is managed by the provider, so the check is on Cloud Audit Logs configuration rather than a cluster object.

Decision needed: no

### old-8eef92: gke-stockout-investigator

The agent is pointed at a kube-agents repository path inside the GitOps working copy, where it does not exist, so the direct lookup fails and the agent falls back to a broad search or guesses.

**Recommendation** (likely): The workspace is the install's GitOps repository, not a kube-agents checkout; drop the fixed path and keep only the search_files hint (or name the repository's own manifest directory).

### old-cef70d: gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)

The documented gcloud flag does not exist, so an agent that runs the step as written gets an unrecognized-arguments error.

Evidence:
- `gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)/SKILL.md:117`: "    --enable-dataplane-v2-observability \"

**Recommendation** (confident): gcloud container clusters update has no --enable-dataplane-v2-observability flag; the current flags are --enable-dataplane-v2-flow-observability (flow visibility) and --enable-dataplane-v2-metrics (flow metrics), as the platform copy of this skill already uses.

Decision needed: no

### old-5539c7: gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)

The audit step tells the agent to look for fields that the quoted command's --format filter never prints, so the agent either reports them missing or re-runs the describe with a guess.

Evidence:
- `gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)/SKILL.md:19`: "gcloud container clusters describe <cluster-name> --region <region> --project <project-id> --format="json(loggingConfig, monitoringConfig)""
- `gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)/SKILL.md:22`: "Look for `loggingService` and `monitoringService` to be set to something other than `none` (usually `logging.googleapis.com/kubernetes` and `monitoring.googleapis.com/kubernetes`)."

**Recommendation** (likely): On the current GKE cluster resource loggingService and monitoringService are top-level fields while loggingConfig/monitoringConfig carry componentConfig.enableComponents and managedPrometheusConfig; either add the two service fields to the json() projection or tell the agent to read enableComponents.

Decision needed: no

### old-f4a3fa: gke-reliability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-reliability)

An agent that runs the maintenance-window command as written gets a gcloud argument error, because `--maintenance-window-start` must be paired with `--maintenance-window-end` or `--maintenance-window-duration`.

Evidence:
- `gke-reliability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-reliability)/SKILL.md:93`: "    --maintenance-window-start <start-time> \"
- `gke-reliability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-reliability)/SKILL.md:94`: "    --maintenance-window-recurrence "FREQ=DAILY""

**Recommendation** (confident): gcloud's flag group is `--maintenance-window-recurrence --maintenance-window-start (--maintenance-window-duration | --maintenance-window-end)`, so add `--maintenance-window-end <end-time>` (or a duration) to the example.

Decision needed: no

### old-898ced: gke-storage (agents/cluster/skills/gke-storage)

A PVC that names `standard-rwm` never binds, because GKE's pre-defined Filestore StorageClasses are `standard-rwx`, `premium-rwx`, `enterprise-rwx` and `zonal-rwx`; the platform copy of this skill and the operator's test manifests both use `standard-rwx`.

Evidence:
- `gke-storage (agents/cluster/skills/gke-storage)/SKILL.md:55`: "  storageClassName: standard-rwm # Pre-defined for Filestore"

**Recommendation** (likely): The Filestore Basic HDD class is `standard-rwx`; change the example to that name.

Decision needed: no

### old-db1a0f: gke-workload-troubleshooting (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-troubleshooting)

Step 0 sends the agent to `SETTINGS.md` and to `gcloud container clusters get-credentials` before diagnostics, but the Cluster Agent now gets its identity from USER.md with a pinned KUBECONFIG and must run cluster_preflight.sh first, so the step is redundant at best and points at the wrong source of truth.

Evidence:
- `gke-workload-troubleshooting (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-troubleshooting)/SKILL.md:14`: "To begin troubleshooting, acquire the following context from the user or active `SETTINGS.md` config:"

**Recommendation** (likely): What is true now (agents/cluster/AGENTS.md): project, cluster and location are fixed in USER.md, KUBECONFIG is pinned at scaffold time, and `bash /opt/data/scripts/cluster_preflight.sh --json` is the required pre-diagnostics step, so Step 0 should read the identity from USER.md and rely on the preflight instead of fetching credentials.

### old-d507a6: github-issue-resolver

An agent told to call a tool named write_to_file will not find it in the Hermes file toolset and may fall back to writing the report through the shell, which the skill explicitly tries to avoid.

Evidence:
- `github-issue-resolver/SKILL.md:141`: "`write_to_file` tool to write your formatted Markdown report to"

**Recommendation** (likely): The Hermes file toolset exposes write_file (agents/chat/config.yaml line 243 lists read_file / write_file / patch / search_files, and bench and deploy patches reference write_file); rename the tool in the skill to write_file.

Decision needed: no

### old-3f8cf5: fleet-upgrade-verification

Line 24 calls the drift-detection design 'not yet shipped'. docs/designs/drift-detection.md (STATUS line) says 'partially implemented': terraform/modules/drift-pubsub and k8s-operator/cmd/drift-detector exist, the detector is built into the agent images and starts only when the PlatformAgent sets spec.harness.driftDetector.enabled; the same STATUS line also says to treat the design as 'not as shipped behaviour'. The parenthetical is therefore imprecise rather than wrong: the component ships dormant, the design as a whole does not. An agent that repeats 'not yet shipped' to an operator who has enabled the detector gives a stale answer; on a stock install the statement still holds.

Evidence:
- `fleet-upgrade-verification/SKILL.md:24`: "live peers, and the drift-detection design (not yet shipped) means live state diverging from Git."

**Recommendation** (unsure): Replace '(not yet shipped)' with wording that matches the design doc's STATUS line, such as '(partially implemented; the detector runs only when the install sets spec.harness.driftDetector.enabled)'. The line predates the change under review (the pull request does not touch it; it moved from line 22 to 24), so this is a follow-up, not a regression of the change.

Decision needed: yes

### old-a613c8: kube-agents-observability

All five helper scripts mint a token with `gcloud auth application-default print-access-token`, which the credential broker that fronts gcloud in the Platform Agent refuses (policy rule gcp.access-token-disclosure, pattern `gcloud ... auth ... print-(access|identity)-token`), so every scripted step in the skill fails on the install it ships to, and the Cloud Trace and Cloud Logging calls have no API relay route at all.

Evidence:
- `kube-agents-observability/scripts/check_token_usage.py:24`: "    token = subprocess.check_output(['gcloud', 'auth', 'application-default', 'print-access-token']).decode().strip()"
- `kube-agents-observability/scripts/get_metric_descriptors.py:19`: "    token = subprocess.check_output(['gcloud', 'auth', 'application-default', 'print-access-token']).decode().strip()"
- `kube-agents-observability/scripts/fetch_traces.py:26`: "    token = subprocess.check_output(['gcloud', 'auth', 'application-default', 'print-access-token']).decode().strip()"
- `kube-agents-observability/scripts/analyze_trace_latency.py:29`: "    token = subprocess.check_output(['gcloud', 'auth', 'application-default', 'print-access-token']).decode().strip()"
- `kube-agents-observability/scripts/get_chat_users.py:24`: "    token = subprocess.check_output(['gcloud', 'auth', 'application-default', 'print-access-token']).decode().strip()"
- `kube-agents-observability/SKILL.md:85`: "Send this PromQL through the credential broker's API relay (`credential_proxy_client.ApiSession`, route `gcp.api.monitoring.promql-read`); the relay carries the credential, so the query needs no token of its own."

**Recommendation** (confident): What is true now: the agent reaches GCP read APIs only through credential_proxy_client.ApiSession and the routes in agents/platform/scripts/api_policy.py (monitoring timeSeries, metricDescriptors, PromQL), so rewrite check_token_usage.py and get_metric_descriptors.py onto ApiSession as fleet_waste.py does, and either add relay routes for cloudtrace.googleapis.com and logging.googleapis.com or mark the trace and chat-user scripts as runnable only from an operator workstation with ADC.

Decision needed: yes

### old-493eb0: fleet-upgrade-verification

The skill's own text counts the kubectl reads after the PDB read two different ways. Lines 213 to 218 list four groups (daemonset/cronjob/pvc/networkpolicy/namespace; pods; nodes; storageclass/pv), scripts/fleet_upgrade_report.py has four LATER_READS entries and its --readiness help prints 1 + 4 = 5 kubectl reads per member, but line 259 says a failed get-credentials or PDB read skips 'the three reads after it'. An agent that reads the later paragraph tells the operator three reads were skipped when the script skips four. Lines 224 to 225 ('A rule that needs more reads / it through the script's own runner') are a complete sentence (a rule that needs more, reads it through the runner) but 'more reads' first parses as a noun phrase, so a reader has to backtrack; that is a readability nit, not a broken sentence.

Evidence:
- `fleet-upgrade-verification/SKILL.md:259`: "needs an object ungraded (the three reads after it are skipped, since the API server did not"
- `fleet-upgrade-verification/SKILL.md:213`: "the PDB read, one per group of kinds: `kubectl get daemonset,cronjob,pvc,networkpolicy,namespace"
- `fleet-upgrade-verification/scripts/fleet_upgrade_report.py:282`: "LATER_READS = ("
- `fleet-upgrade-verification/scripts/fleet_upgrade_report.py:288`: "KUBECTL_READS_PER_MEMBER = 1 + len(LATER_READS)"
- `fleet-upgrade-verification/SKILL.md:224`: "so nothing after it ran). A rule that needs more reads"
- `fleet-upgrade-verification/SKILL.md:225`: "it through the script's own runner, under the same per-call cap. The audit-log reader beside the"
- `fleet-upgrade-verification/scripts/fleet_upgrade_report.py:1365`: "{KUBECTL_READS_PER_MEMBER} kubectl reads per member in all, each after the first failing on its own"

**Recommendation** (confident): Patch SKILL.md: change 'the three reads after it' at line 259 to 'the four reads after it' so it matches the four-group list at lines 213 to 218, the four entries of LATER_READS and the script's own help count. Optionally reword lines 224 to 225 to 'A rule that needs more reads it itself through the script's own runner' (or add a comma after 'more') so the sentence does not garden-path. Both lines were added by the change under review, so the fix belongs in the same pull request.

Decision needed: no

### old-9cab9f: gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)

The appended section describes the fleet-upgrade-verification script more narrowly than that skill does. gke-upgrades says that without --readiness the script 'reads with gcloud container only' and 'the only thing it then writes is its own record'; fleet-upgrade-verification (and the script: PROJECTS_LIST_CMD, CONFIG_PROJECT_CMD) say the same run, with no --project or declared scope, also runs gcloud config get-value project and gcloud projects list, and that the --output file, which gke-upgrades' own example passes, is written too. The two accounts agree on what matters most (read-only, nothing changed in GCP); the drift is which gcloud groups run and the --output file, so an agent quoting gke-upgrades alone understates the reads and omits one written file. These lines predate the change under review; the change only widened the readiness paragraph after them.

Evidence:
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/SKILL.md:234`: "completed or stalled since the previous run. Without `--readiness` (below) it reads with"
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/SKILL.md:235`: "`gcloud container` only and changes nothing in GCP; the only thing it then writes is its own"
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/SKILL.md:229`: "  --output /opt/data/scratch/fleet_versions.json"
- `gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades)/../../skill-overlays/gke-upgrades/append.md:19`: "`gcloud container` only and changes nothing in GCP; the only thing it then writes is its own"
- `fleet-upgrade-verification/SKILL.md:59`: "`--project` nor a declared scope is passed, `gcloud config get-value project` and `gcloud projects list` (plus `gcloud projects describe` for a"
- `fleet-upgrade-verification/SKILL.md:68`: "needs, and the `--output` file. A failed or timed-out gcloud read, `get-credentials`, PDB read"

**Recommendation** (likely): Patch the overlay (agents/platform/skill-overlays/gke-upgrades/append.md, lines 18 to 20) to say the script reads with gcloud container, and gcloud config and gcloud projects when no project or scope is passed, and writes its record and the --output file; then run make skills-refresh so the shipped SKILL.md follows. Keep the two sentences short and let fleet-upgrade-verification own the full list.

Decision needed: no

These skills cover the same ground: 0 or 1 star in the coverage matrix (see the
appendix). Keeping both wastes the agent's attention and lets them drift apart.

### dup-60a3d0: cluster-agent-lifecycle, manage-cluster

Both Platform Agent skills describe creating a Cluster Agent profile with the same command, and they already disagree on one detail (where the cluster identity is written: "into the profile config" vs "into USER.md"); a reader following one gets a different picture than a reader following the other.

Evidence:
- `manage-cluster/SKILL.md:26`: "python3 /opt/data/scripts/cluster_agent_profile.py create \"
- `cluster-agent-lifecycle/SKILL.md:19`: "python3 /opt/data/scripts/cluster_agent_profile.py create \"
- `manage-cluster/SKILL.md:3`: "description: Bring an existing GKE cluster under management on user request (e.g. "manage my cluster <name> in <location>") by creating its Cluster Agent profile. Use whenever a user asks to manage/on"
- `cluster-agent-lifecycle/SKILL.md:3`: "description: Create, delegate to, and tear down per-cluster Cluster Agent Hermes profiles. Use whenever a GKE cluster is onboarded or deleted, or whenever a single-cluster runtime debugging/operations"

**Recommendation** (unsure): Keep both: cluster-agent-lifecycle hands the user-driven case to manage-cluster, which has verify and un-manage steps the other lacks, and SOUL.md and AGENTS.md route "manage my cluster" to it. Tidy instead of removing: state the split in both descriptions and fix the one drift so both say where the identity is written.

Decision needed: yes

### dup-b7e664: gke-reliability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-reliability), gke-reliability (agents/platform/skills/gke-reliability)

Two shipped copies of gke-reliability with the same name load into different profiles: the Cluster Agent copy is a hand-maintained snapshot of the pre-sync text, the Platform Agent copy is generated from upstream plus overlays. They have already drifted (see the conflicts section) and the cluster copy is written for a persona that can apply changes, which the Cluster Agent cannot.

Evidence:

**Recommendation** (likely): Keep the Platform Agent copy as the shipped source of truth. Rewrite the cluster copy as a short read-only diagnostic skill (describe maintenancePolicy, list PDBs and probe coverage) that hands fixes to the Platform Agent, or remove it and let the Cluster Agent escalate.

Decision needed: yes

### low-7b2b60: review-security-k8s-agents-prompt-injection

The architecture docs (docs/architecture/04-workflow-model.md section 3) make this review suite the only gate for prompt-injection risk, yet the skill holds two generic bullets, a lone section heading numbered 1 with no section 2, and no check for the input-sanitization coverage its description promises, so an orchestrator that invokes it gets almost no prompt-injection coverage.

Evidence:
- `review-security-k8s-agents-prompt-injection/SKILL.md:3`: "description: Reviews AI agent architectures (API gateways, WAFs, input sanitization) for prompt injection risks."
- `review-security-k8s-agents-prompt-injection/SKILL.md:9`: "## 1. Input Sanitization & Proxies"
- `review-security-k8s-agents-prompt-injection/SKILL.md:10`: "- **Gateway/WAF**: Require LLM-specific API Gateway or WAF sidecar. Flag raw agent APIs exposed to untrusted traffic."
- `review-security-k8s-agents-prompt-injection/SKILL.md:11`: "- **Guardrails**: Ensure system prompts/safety instructions in `ConfigMaps`/`EnvVars` cannot be tampered with by less privileged workloads."

**Recommendation** (likely): Extend the skill with concrete checks for the prompt-injection paths an agent on Kubernetes actually faces: indirect injection through cluster data the agent reads (resource names, annotations, logs, events, issue and PR text), untrusted tool output, tool allowlists and human approval before write actions, and output filtering, to match the depth of the sibling agents-* skills.

Decision needed: yes

### place-ac2429: gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)

A read-only persona is handed cluster-mutating gcloud update steps; the Cluster Agent is forbidden from running them, and the Platform Agent already carries the newer upstream copy that owns configuration.

Evidence:
- `gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)/SKILL.md:3`: "description: Workflows for setting up and auditing observability (logging, monitoring, tracing) on GKE."
- `gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)/SKILL.md:31`: "gcloud container clusters update <cluster-name> \"
- `gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)/SKILL.md:32`: "    --enable-managed-prometheus \"
- `gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)/SKILL.md:104`: "gcloud container clusters update <cluster-name> \"
- `gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)/SKILL.md:105`: "    --monitoring=SYSTEM,API_SERVER,SCHEDULER,CONTROLLER_MANAGER \"
- `gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)/SKILL.md:116`: "gcloud container clusters update <cluster-name> \"
- `gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)/SKILL.md:117`: "    --enable-dataplane-v2-observability \"
- `gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability)/../../SOUL.md:14`: "- **Read-Only Boundary:** You are strictly forbidden from mutating cluster state. Do not `kubectl apply`, `patch`, `edit`, `delete`, `scale`, `rollout restart`, or `exec` into workloads. Your terminal and tools are for read-only diagnostics: `get`, `describe`, `logs`, `events`, `top`, and equivalent read-only reads. All remediation flows through the Platform Agent."

**Recommendation** (likely): Keep only the read-only audit and query workflows (sections 1, 3, 7 and the LQL examples) in the cluster copy and leave enabling Managed Prometheus, control-plane metrics and Dataplane V2 observability to agents/platform/skills/gke-observability.

Decision needed: yes

### place-3b4682: gke-reliability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-reliability)

The skill is framed as configuring the cluster and ends with a `gcloud container clusters update` mutation, while agents/cluster/AGENTS.md declares the Cluster Agent read-only (no apply, patch, edit) and offers no read-only procedure for the storage-free HA review the persona actually performs.

Evidence:
- `gke-reliability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-reliability)/SKILL.md:8`: "This skill provides workflows for configuring your GKE cluster and workloads for high availability and reliability."
- `gke-reliability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-reliability)/SKILL.md:91`: "gcloud container clusters update <cluster-name> \"

**Recommendation** (unsure): Reframe the cluster copy as read-only HA checks (describe maintenancePolicy, list PDBs and probe coverage) whose output is a proposed manifest in the kanban result, and drop the `clusters update` command, which belongs to the platform copy.

Decision needed: yes

### place-007b88: gke-storage (agents/cluster/skills/gke-storage)

Every workflow in the skill is a write (create a StorageClass, edit and apply a PVC to resize it) and none is a diagnostic, so under the read-only Cluster Agent (agents/cluster/AGENTS.md: never apply, patch or edit) it gives the agent nothing for the PVC/storage-binding debugging that agents/cluster/CAPABILITIES.md routes to it, while the platform copy already carries the configuration content.

Evidence:
- `gke-storage (agents/cluster/skills/gke-storage)/SKILL.md:3`: "description: Guidance on managing storage in Google Kubernetes Engine (GKE) clusters."
- `gke-storage (agents/cluster/skills/gke-storage)/SKILL.md:67`: "1. Edit the PVC manifest and increase the storage request."
- `gke-storage (agents/cluster/skills/gke-storage)/SKILL.md:68`: "2. Apply the changes."

**Recommendation** (likely): Rewrite the cluster copy as a read-only storage-binding diagnosis (PVC phase and events, StorageClass and CSI driver presence, PV node affinity, pending-pod mount errors) that ends in a proposed patch in the kanban result.

Decision needed: yes

### place-a8b7f5: gke-workload-scaling (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-scaling)

The Cluster Agent is forbidden to mutate cluster state (agents/cluster/AGENTS.md: never `apply`, `scale`, ...), yet every workflow in this copy is a mutation, so the skill either tempts the agent across its red line or gives it nothing it may use.

Evidence:
- `gke-workload-scaling (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-scaling)/SKILL.md:19`: "kubectl scale deployment <deployment-name> --replicas=<number> -n <namespace>"
- `gke-workload-scaling (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-scaling)/SKILL.md:42`: "kubectl apply -f assets/hpa-example.yaml"
- `gke-workload-scaling (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-scaling)/SKILL.md:61`: "gcloud container clusters update <cluster-name> --enable-vertical-pod-autoscaling --zone <zone>"
- `gke-workload-scaling (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-scaling)/SKILL.md:81`: "gcloud container clusters update <cluster-name> \"

**Recommendation** (confident): Rewrite this copy as a read-only autoscaling diagnostics skill (`kubectl get/describe hpa`, `kubectl describe vpa` recommendations, HPA conditions and events, the upstream Rightsizing read step) and drop the scale/apply/gcloud-update steps and the Cluster Autoscaler enable section, which belong to the platform copy.

Decision needed: yes

### place-6816e5: gke-workload-security (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-security)

Seven of the eight workflows create, annotate, label or apply resources or run `gcloud container clusters update`, which the read-only Cluster Agent persona forbids, so only the audit script and the dataplane describe check are usable where this copy ships.

Evidence:
- `gke-workload-security (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:3`: "description: Workflows for auditing and hardening the security of GKE workloads."
- `gke-workload-security (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:44`: "kubectl create namespace workload-identity-test-ns"
- `gke-workload-security (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:70`: "kubectl apply -f ./assets/workload-identity-pod.yaml -n workload-identity-test-ns"
- `gke-workload-security (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:106`: "gcloud container clusters update <cluster-name> \"
- `gke-workload-security (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-security)/SKILL.md:159`: "kubectl label --overwrite ns <namespace> \"

**Recommendation** (likely): Keep the audit script and the read-only posture checks in the cluster copy and leave the hardening steps to agents/platform/skills/gke-workload-security, which already carries them.

Decision needed: yes

### fm-212e6f: fleet-audit-reports

A strict YAML parser rejects the front matter (mapping values are not allowed here, line 2 column 313), so a runtime that does not fall back to a first-colon split skips the skill.

Evidence:
- `fleet-audit-reports/SKILL.md:3`: "description: Answer a question about a past autonomous fleet audit from the on-pod report store — what a stream last found, how many criticals are open, what changed between two runs, which clusters were skipped or only partly covered, and when each stream last ran. Never the answer to a request to run an audit: a run collects, publishes and opens its fixes through the fleet-audit skill."

**Recommendation** (confident): Quote the description string (or use a YAML block scalar) so the unquoted ": " inside it stops being read as a nested mapping.

Decision needed: no

## 8. Proposed changes

These are safe and the pull request applies them:

- Patch gke-workload-scaling (agents/platform/skills/gke-workload-scaling), gke-batch-hpc (agents/platform/skills/gke-batch-hpc), gke-cluster-creation (agents/platform/skills/gke-cluster-creation), gke-networking (agents/platform/skills/gke-networking), gke-platform-security (agents/platform/skills/gke-platform-security), gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades), gke-workload-identity (agents/platform/skills/gke-workload-identity) (Edit line 1 of the seven agents/platform/skill-overlays/<skill>/append.md files, then make skills-refresh.) [old-1a00d3]
- Patch review-security-k8s-agents-audit-logs (Replace `AuditPolicy` with `Policy` (`audit.k8s.io/v1`), point at `k8s-operator/config/audit/audit-policy.yaml` as the reference, and add the GKE note that the audit policy is not user-editable there.) [old-cae437]
- Patch gke-stockout-investigator (Replace the `<workspace>/agents/platform/skills/gke-compute-classes/assets/` hint on line 137 with a search of the GitOps workspace for ComputeClass manifests (`search_files(pattern="kind: ComputeClass")`), since that path belongs to this repository and not to a user's GitOps repository.) [old-8eef92]
- Update gke-observability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-observability) (Change the format to `--format="json(loggingService, monitoringService, loggingConfig, monitoringConfig)"` and say that `loggingConfig.componentConfig.enableComponents` / `monitoringConfig.componentConfig.enableComponents` and `monitoringConfig.managedPrometheusConfig.enabled` are what to inspect, as the platform copy's defaults table does.; Replace `--enable-dataplane-v2-observability` with `--enable-dataplane-v2-flow-observability` (and mention `--enable-dataplane-v2-metrics` for metrics only), matching agents/platform/skills/gke-observability/SKILL.md line 63.) [old-cef70d, old-5539c7]
- Update gke-reliability (agents/cluster/skills/gke-reliability) (Add `--maintenance-window-end <end-time> \` between the start and recurrence flags in the section 6 command block.) [old-f4a3fa]
- Update gke-storage (agents/cluster/skills/gke-storage) (Replace `standard-rwm` with `standard-rwx` on line 55.) [old-898ced]
- Update gke-workload-troubleshooting (agents/cluster/skills/gke-workload-troubleshooting) (Rewrite Step 0 to take project/cluster/location from USER.md and the card, remove the mandatory get-credentials command and the SETTINGS.md reference, and point at the preflight script.) [old-db1a0f]
- Update github-issue-resolver (Replace `write_to_file` with `write_file` in Step 4 of agents/platform/skills/github-issue-resolver/SKILL.md.) [old-d507a6]
- Update install-kube-agents (In the GitOps section, scope the GitHub Token Minter paths and the organization rule to `--gitops-forge=github` (the default), add a short GitLab paragraph linking docs/site/src/content/docs/install/gitlab.md, and add the `--gitops-forge`, `--gitops-host`, `--gitlab-token-file`, `--gitlab-token-secret`, `--gitops-ca-file` rows to the flag table.) [old-9199dc]
- Patch kube-agents-observability (Change "for the Chat Agent" to "for the Planning Agent (the `default` profile)" on SKILL.md line 129.) [old-c3b663]
- Patch fleet-audit-reports (Wrap the description value in quotes in agents/platform/skills/fleet-audit-reports/SKILL.md.) [fm-212e6f]
- Patch fleet-upgrade-verification (Fix the read count at SKILL.md line 259 (three -> four); optionally reword the garden-path sentence at lines 224-225.) [old-493eb0]
- Patch gke-upgrades (agents/platform/skills/gke-upgrades) (Edit the overlay append.md, not the generated SKILL.md, then make skills-refresh.) [old-9cab9f]

## 9. How to respond

Each item marked "needs decision" has an ID like `conf-81be44`. Reply with the
ID and your choice, either as a comment on the pull request or by adding a line
to `.sss/decisions.yaml`:

```yaml
dup-3f9a2c: accept
conf-81be44:
  choice: 3
  note: "Both are right in different regions."
```

The next run reads those answers and applies them. Items you have answered are
not asked again.

## Appendix: every skill we found

One entry per skill. "Goal" is what the skill is for, in one line. "Concerns"
lists every finding above that involves it.

### edit-mirrored-skill

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/edit-mirrored-skill`
- **Goal:** Tells a developer how to change, update, or adopt one of the gke-* skills that this repo copies from google/skills, so the local change survives future upstream syncs and the repo's consistency check passes.
- **Concerns:** none

### install-kube-agents

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/install-kube-agents`
- **Goal:** Guides an agent through installing kube-agents and its Platform Agent onto a GKE cluster in a GCP project by detecting the environment, interviewing the operator, previewing with a dry run, and applying only after confirmation.
- **Concerns:**
  - out of date [old-9199dc]: `install.sh` now accepts `--gitops-forge=github|gitlab` plus `--gitops-host`, `--gitlab-token-file`, `--gitlab-token-secret` and `--gitops-…

### review-address-feedback

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-address-feedback`
- **Goal:** Guides a developer or agent through answering every review comment on an open kube-agents pull request until the bot review is settled and the change can merge.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-adversarial

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-adversarial`
- **Goal:** Gives a reviewer a fixed procedure to find and defend real defects in a code change before it merges, by reading the diff from ten angles and re-checking every suspected problem against the source.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-docs-drift

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-docs-drift`
- **Goal:** Checks a pull request to find documentation that the change made inaccurate, verifies changed docs against the source of truth, and reports which documents need updating.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-preflight

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-preflight`
- **Goal:** Before a pull request is opened, run the two required review passes (adversarial and docs-drift) in fresh contexts that did not write the change, and merge their results into the PR's Self-Review disposition list.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-admission

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-admission`
- **Goal:** Reviews Kubernetes admission control resources (validating and mutating webhooks, ValidatingAdmissionPolicy, MutatingAdmissionPolicy) for bypass, scope-evasion, TLS, and mutation-ordering weaknesses.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-agents-audit-logs

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-agents-audit-logs`
- **Goal:** Reviews Kubernetes and application logging for AI agent workloads so that every agent API call, prompt, output, and tool invocation is recorded in a way the agent cannot tamper with.
- **Concerns:**
  - out of date [old-cae437]: Name the resource as it exists: a `kind: Policy` object in `apiVersion: audit.k8s.io/v1` (the repo's reference is `k8s-operator/config/audi…

### review-security-k8s-agents-credentials

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-agents-credentials`
- **Goal:** Reviews Kubernetes configuration for AI agents so that agent containers never hold credentials directly: secrets go through a credential proxy sidecar, service-account tokens are not auto-mounted, and RBAC is tightly scoped.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-agents-data-exfil

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-agents-data-exfil`
- **Goal:** Reviews Kubernetes network and access configuration to stop an AI agent, for example one under prompt injection, from sending data out: egress allowlists, egress gateways, and limits on reading secrets and ConfigMaps.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-agents-firewall

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-agents-firewall`
- **Goal:** Reviews NetworkPolicies and firewall rules around AI agent control loops and execution sandboxes: default-deny egress, blocked access to cluster APIs and the cloud metadata server, and restricted ingress to agent endpoints.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-agents-main

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-agents-main`
- **Goal:** Orchestrates an AI-agent-focused Kubernetes security audit by running the sandbox, firewall, credentials, prompt-injection, data-exfiltration, and audit-log review skills in parallel, triaging their findings against project context, and merging them into one JSON report.
- **Concerns:**
  - duplicate with review-security-k8s-main [dup-5cf701]: Keep both. No change.

### review-security-k8s-agents-prompt-injection

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-agents-prompt-injection`
- **Goal:** Checks whether an AI agent deployment puts an LLM-aware gateway or WAF in front of its API and protects its system prompts in ConfigMaps or environment variables from tampering by less privileged workloads.
- **Concerns:**
  - low value [low-7b2b60]: Extend the skill with concrete checks for the prompt-injection paths an agent on Kubernetes actually faces: indirect injection through clus… Decision needed.

### review-security-k8s-agents-sandbox

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-agents-sandbox`
- **Goal:** Checks that the code-execution sandboxes an AI agent uses (REPLs, shell tools, WASM) run on a hardened RuntimeClass, are isolated from the agent loop with seccomp or AppArmor, are disposable, and carry tight resource limits.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-gateway

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-gateway`
- **Goal:** Checks Kubernetes Gateway API resources (Gateway, HTTPRoute, TCPRoute, TLSRoute, ReferenceGrant) for route hijacking, unscoped cross-namespace references, weak TLS listeners, open allowedRoutes, and wildcard hostnames.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-main

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-main`
- **Goal:** Runs a full Kubernetes security review by gathering project context, launching the per-topic review-security-k8s-* sub-skills in parallel, filtering their findings against the context, and merging them into one JSON report.
- **Concerns:**
  - duplicate with review-security-k8s-agents-main [dup-5cf701]: Keep both. No change.

### review-security-k8s-namespaces

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-namespaces`
- **Goal:** Checks namespace layout for isolation problems: too many workloads in one namespace, mixed trust levels, custom workloads in system namespaces, look-alike namespace names, policy-bypass labels, oversized quotas, odd finalizers, cross-namespace references, and leftover secrets or bindings in empty namespaces.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-network

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-network`
- **Goal:** Checks NetworkPolicies, Services, Ingresses and service-mesh policies for missing default-deny, no egress limits, broad CIDRs, public LoadBalancers, NodePorts, sensitive ports, missing Ingress TLS, manual Endpoints, hostNetwork, and non-STRICT mTLS.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-nodes

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-nodes`
- **Goal:** Checks manifests for ways a pod or node could escalate to the whole cluster: hostPath mounts of kubelet credentials or runtime sockets, extra RBAC for system:nodes, node impersonation, node-modifying roles, and broad tolerations that let untrusted pods land on sensitive nodes.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-pod

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-pod`
- **Goal:** Gives a reviewer a checklist for auditing Pod and container securityContext settings (privileged mode, host namespaces, hostPath, capabilities, root, seccomp, service-account tokens, image digests) for privilege-escalation and node-breakout risks.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-rbac

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-rbac`
- **Goal:** Gives a reviewer a checklist for auditing Kubernetes Roles, ClusterRoles and bindings for over-broad or escalating permissions, backed by a saved copy of the GKE RBAC best-practices guide.
- **Concerns:** none

### review-security-k8s-service-accounts

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `.agents/skills/review-security-k8s-service-accounts`
- **Goal:** Gives a reviewer a checklist for auditing Kubernetes ServiceAccounts: default-account use, one account per app, token automounting, Workload Identity sharing, long-lived token Secrets, imagePullSecrets and orphaned accounts.

### manage-cluster

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `agents/platform/skills/manage-cluster`

- **Concerns:**
  - duplicate with cluster-agent-lifecycle [dup-60a3d0]: Keep both: cluster-agent-lifecycle hands the user-driven case to manage-cluster, which has verify and un-manage steps the other lacks, and… Decision needed.

### pr-conversation

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `agents/platform/skills/pr-conversation`
- **Goal:** Tells the Platform Agent how to answer a reviewer who addressed it on one of its own pull requests: re-read the thread with pr_conversation.py poll, answer or amend the branch, verify the commit, and post a reply or refusal that marks the request handled.
- **Concerns:** none

### submit-suggestion

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `agents/platform/skills/submit-suggestion`
- **Goal:** Lets the Platform Agent propose a configuration change by preparing a working copy of the GitOps repository, committing files on a platform-agent/ branch and opening or updating a pull request (merge request on GitLab) through the broker, including a second round that addresses review feedback.
- **Concerns:** none

### version-control

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `agents/platform/skills/version-control`
- **Goal:** Lets the Platform Agent read, change, and discuss a hosted Git repository (history, branches, pull requests, issues, labels) without ever holding a forge credential or a forge-specific CLI.
- **Concerns:** none

### workload-rebalancing

- **Source:** local  **Status:** unmanaged  **Location:** `agents/platform/skills/workload-rebalancing`
- **Goal:** Guides the Platform Agent to move a workload from an overloaded cluster to one with headroom by fanning out read-only kanban validation cards to both Cluster Agents and then declaring the move as a single GitOps pull request via submit-suggestion.
- **Concerns:** none
